AI-INFRA · LEADING-INDICATORS COMMAND CENTER · IBKR-TRADABLE

What's growing faster than qualified supply — and the market hasn't priced yet

A MECE leading-indicator map of the full AI-infrastructure stack — demand acceleration, supply shortages, bottleneck rotation, margin risk, deployment constraints — scored with free leading data and staged on the awareness curve. As-of 2026-08-03. Personal research, NOT investment advice. IBKR read-only.

The agent's standing question, asked of every node: "What component, capacity, or infrastructure requirement is growing faster than qualified supply, and has the market not yet priced it in?"
Market scope — IBKR-AVAILABLE markets (policy mp_v2, 2026-07-01): US/ADR + JP + KR + EU + TW local lines are all directly recommendable; CN mainland (SZ/SS) stays research-context only. TW rows carry a verify-account-access note until TWSE permission is confirmed in the actual IBKR account. US-anchored data (EDGAR + Yahoo); non-US names carry local-market context. Policy history: curated research notes written before 2026-07-01 may still say 'TW = context-only' — that was the pre-mp_v2 rule; such phrases in dated research prose are historical, not current policy.
白話版. 這是一張「AI 基礎建設」的全棧領先指標地圖：把整個 AI 機櫃從需求（雲端資本支出）→供給（GPU/ASIC、CPU、記憶體、先進封裝、被動元件、電源矽、網通光通訊、伺服器）→部署（液冷、電網/變壓器）→獲利/利潤風險，用 MECE 拆成四大透鏡、12 個次產業、再到個股。每個節點問同一句話：哪裡的「合格供給」跟不上需求，而股價還沒反應？ 只看你 IBKR 能買的市場（美股為主、ADR、歐日韓），台股標的只作供應鏈脈絡參考、標記「無法交易」並導向可買的替代。右下角的 alpha 象限＝營收還在加速、但股價位階還低。資料分兩層：可計算層（SEC EDGAR 財報加速度、Yahoo 股價位階與相對強弱、DigiKey/Mouser 零件交期）＋研究層（每週由獨立代理人查證、附來源），並由 Codex 扮演散戶做對抗式評審、每週自我演進。

Bottleneck rotation — where the binding constraint sits now

Nodes ranked by net pressure = tightness − 0.6 × priced-in (where a forming shortage is least reflected in price). The framework's core alpha: catch the constraint as it rotates.

Power Equipment / Grid / Dnet 4.8tight 9 · priced 7▲ rotating in

Networking & Opticsnet 3.8tight 8 · priced 7▼ easing/priced
Capex-Conversion & Margin net 3.8tight 8 · priced 7▲ rotating in

The binding constraint did not rotate this week — it got QUANTIFIED, which matters more. Applied Digital's 10-K (2026-07-29) shows 1,410 MW contracted against ~100 MW energized: a 7% conversion rate that puts a number on the system's chokepoint, energized deliverable megawatts, and four nodes now defer to it rather than to themselves (CLOUD names it outright, COOL says explicitly that its own capacity adds will hit an interconnection wall rather than a fabrication wall, SERVER routes its best pick to power content as the next rotation, MARGIN routes to the financing of it). Jassy confirming on 2026-07-30 that AWS is short in both 2026 AND 2027 with most 2027 capacity already reserved, Eaton's Electrical Global backlog more than doubling, Quanta at a record $53.4B, and Vertiv MISSING its own revenue guide by ~$76M on supply-chain congestion while raising full-year guidance — that is a shortage that has stopped stretching lead times and started destroying revenue recognition. The trap for a retail investor this week is timing, not thesis: nine of twelve nodes cut their `priced` score on the July de-rate, but verified on 2026-08-03 that de-rate is already reversing hard (CRWV +13.6%, AXTI +14.8%, APLD +8.2%, IREN +6.9%, VRT +6.6%), and Eaton is back at a 52-week high having gained 14% in five sessions — so most of the "it got cheaper" alpha this run describes prices that no longer exist. What survives that test is narrow and specific: Hubbell, +7.11% YTD and DOWN 5.59% over six months at ~23x raised guidance while the identical end-market trades at 32-40x, is the one owner of the binding constraint the AI multiple never touched; Amkor at -47.7% from its high and below its 200-day is a record-printing, guidance-raising OSAT contracted to both TSMC Arizona and NVIDIA, sitting in the 2.5D/3D packaging queue that PKG, ACCEL and NETOPT independently rotated into (and the real fabrication owners are all Taiwan-listed and unbuyable here); IREN at +3.92% YTD holds contracted powered capacity with ~45% of GPU capex prepaid, which solves the exact funding constraint MARGIN says binds next — though at beta 4.279 and already +15% in five sessions, that window is closing. Rotating out: HBM4 qualification (resolved early, three sources shipping), cold plates and quick-disconnects (the Taiwan owners are near highs, collecting no scarcity rent), the merchant x86 socket (Arm just overtook it in accelerated servers, $53.0B vs $34.6B), the supplier margin-compression bear case (dead — Vertiv raised margin guidance), and memory contract-price momentum (second derivative collapsed from +93-98% to +13-18% QoQ). Hold one honest doubt above all of it: Exelon cut high-probability data-center load ~40% to 11 GW, nVent's backlog fell sequentially for the first time this cycle, and spot DDR5 fell 4.4% w/w against rising contract prices — three independent first-cracks in the week the system scores tightness at 9. This framework treats backlog as leading and price as lagging; if that backlog is partly phantom load now being pruned, the premise inverts. Personal research for a private knowledge base — NOT investment advice, and no trades.

Opportunity analysis — what is worth working next

This layer translates the L3 pressure map into concrete research opportunities: what is already hard-confirmed, what is only expert-research context, which ticker expression is least bad, and exactly what data would upgrade or falsify the setup. It is a prioritization queue, not a trade list.

Opportunity lens: ranks what is worth researching next, not what is action-grade. Rows combine L3 pressure, fired hard-feed alerts, SemiAnalysis-derived context, MOPS/model evidence, price/ticker stage, historical prior context, FMP catalyst/awareness context, and the open blocker. Every row keeps promoted_to_action_gate=false until hard confirmation plus PIT expectations/valuation evidence exists.

Multi-signal TXN bridge — fundamentals + OHLCV technicals + Form 4/13F/N-PORT/13D-G flow context + FTD/short/DSV/ATS/options/IV-gamma context

This is the new agent-prompt bridge: every ticker is scored on a common 0-100 scale for fundamentals, Yahoo RS/52w recognition, Yahoo OHLCV trend/volume/VWAP/breakout confirmation, FMP recognition proxy, SEC Form 4 insider activity, lagged SEC 13F sponsorship, delayed SEC N-PORT fund-holding ownership/crowding context, SEC 13D/G beneficial-ownership context, SEC fails-to-deliver context, FINRA short-interest context, FINRA daily short-sale-volume activity context, FINRA ATS/OTC transparency market-structure context, Nasdaq option-chain call/put context, derived Nasdaq IV/skew/gamma context, crowding/risk, and composite discovery/confirmation. It also emits pipe-delimited TXN|v=2, SCORE|v=2, ALERT|v=2, SUBSECTOR|v=2, and SOURCE_HEALTH|v=2 records. Missing borrow-cost/utilization, true options-flow attribution/dealer positioning, true ETF/fund-flow, N-PORT change history, and foreign-flow feeds stay visible as gaps.

Multi-signal TXN bridge: 94 ticker scores, 11 subsector scores, 1477 pipe-delimited records, and 10 alert records. Exported to multisignal_txn.log, multisignal_scores.csv, and multisignal_subsector_scores.csv. TECH blends Yahoo summary RS/52w position with Yahoo daily OHLCV trend/volume/VWAP/breakout context; FLOW blends FMP analyst-awareness/current-snapshot context with SEC Form 4 insider activity, lagged SEC 13F sponsorship, SEC 13D/G beneficial-ownership context, low-weight SEC XBRL buyback/issuance/share-count context, a low-weight FMP analyst PT/rating revision PIT proxy (NOT EPS vintages, and deliberately excluded from the early-discovery flow-support gate), and a lowest-weight subsector-benchmark ETF creation/redemption implied-share proxy (lumpy/sticky, not company-specific, RULE_7); delayed SEC N-PORT fund-holding exposure is emitted separately as ownership/crowding context and is not part of flow_score. POSITIONING uses SEC fails-to-deliver settlement-pressure context and FINRA short-interest context, while FINRA daily short-sale volume, FINRA ATS/OTC transparency, Nasdaq option-chain call/put data, and derived Nasdaq IV/skew/gamma are separate low-weight activity/convexity/crowding context. RISK uses Yahoo daily OHLCV trailing beta versus SPY/QQQ/SMH/TLT/UUP, liquidity, realized volatility, idiosyncratic volatility, and capital-actions dilution context as validation/falsifier inputs. SEC capital actions are issuer-reported and filing-lagged, not live market sponsorship. Hard borrow, true options-flow attribution/vendor dealer positioning, ETF creations/redemptions, foreign-flow, and N-PORT change history remain source-health gaps.

The AI-rack BOM & deployment cascade

The full stack in physical order (capex → silicon → passives/power → networking → servers → cooling → grid → margin), each node's bar = tightness, the red-outlined node = the current binding constraint. This is the MECE spine; drill into any node below.

tight ≥7 (constraint) building 5–6 early 3–4 loose/easing

The alpha quadrant (live)

Every name with computable fundamentals: x = revenue-acceleration (the leading signal), y = 52-week price position (priced-in). The bottom-right is a computed inflection zone: accelerating fundamentals the price has not fully recognized. It is not automatically an actionable early entry; only the action-gated shortlist can earn that label. Hollow rings = Taiwan-listed (shown for context, not tradable for you). Note: the ^SOX itself is +101% / 6mo, so the whole tape is extended — the edge is in the genuine laggards that are still accelerating.

COMPUTED-INFLECTION (needs action gate) ANCHOR-INFLECTION (accelerating but mega-cap) PRICED-MOMENTUM DECEL-EXIT INFLECTION but untradable

Shortage-cycle staging

Each subsector on the awareness/shortage curve — early tightening (alpha) → confirmed shortage → peak tightness (priced) → easing → oversupply risk.

Hard leading data — distributor lead-times (the automated 2nd source)

Live DigiKey + Mouser part-number lead-times, rolled up to the BOM nodes that have catalog-distributable parts. A real shortage shows up here — in weeks-of-lead-time — before it prints in quarterly revenue. Red >16wk = forming shortage. The weekly cron appends to disti_timeseries.csv, so trend lines build over time (sparklines appear once there are ≥2 weeks).

Feed status: fresh same-run pull from DigiKey + Mouser. Max segment PWR-VRM-CTRL at 61.5wk.

Public Lambda and RunPod pricing pages plus the Azure Retail Prices API now provide an exact free feed for cloud GPU rental prices. The first snapshot proves coverage; the cloud-supply-easing alert only fires after repeated H100 price declines across multiple providers.

Hard leading data — GRID regional deployment constraints

LBNL Queued Up data converts the grid bottleneck from a narrative into measurable regional deployment friction: active requested MW, interconnection-agreement-executed MW, IA share, and queue age. This does not select tickers by itself; it tells the agent where deployment constraints are most severe and whether GRID/POWER watch triggers deserve higher priority.

LBNL GRID deployment constraint: 1864.8GW active requested vs 424.4GW IA-executed (22.8%); median queue age 55.4 months. Source as-of 2025-12-31.

Highest-alpha contradictions

Per the framework, the best signals come from contradictions & bottleneck rotation (capex rising but cloud prices falling; backlog rising but margins falling).

  • THE WHOLE RUN IS MARKED TO A STALE TAPE, and this is mechanically verifiable rather than a judgement call. Nine nodes cut their `priced` score this week citing the July de-rate (ACCEL 8→6, CPU 6→5, PKG 7→5, PASSIVE 8→6, POWER 8→7, COOL 6→5, GRID 9→7, SERVER 8→5, NETOPT 8→7). Verified live on 2026-08-03, that de-rate is violently reversing under the read: CRWV +13.61%, AXTI +14.75%, APLD +8.18%, IREN +6.93%, VRT +6.64%, NVT +4.66%, ETN +4.60%, FN +2.91%. If this bounce holds even a week, most of those priced downgrades — and the alpha they imply — are stale within days. The dashboard is systematically underwriting entries at prices that no longer exist.
  • GRID'S OWN INTERNAL CONTRADICTION: it cut priced from 9 to 7 on the argument that the node de-rated and 'the asymmetry that was spent last week has partially re-opened' — yet its single largest constituent never participated in the de-rate by the time of the read. Eaton closed 2026-08-03 at $434.30 against a $436.74 52-week high, +4.92% on the day and +13.98% over five sessions, YTD +36.77%. The node's priced downgrade is real only for the smaller and offshore names (MYRG -23.25% 1M, POWL, HUBB), not for the mega-caps that dominate the score. Averaging them into one number destroys the signal.
  • THE SAME VERTIV PRINT IS BEING READ THREE INCOMPATIBLE WAYS. GRID uses Vertiv's ~$76M revenue MISS as its single best proof that the shortage has crossed into revenue recognition. COOL uses the identical print as an entry opportunity and adds VRT at #3 while simultaneously stating that COOL's own share of the marginal constraint is DECLINING and that the dashboard-level constraint is POWER/GRID — a bullish pick attached to a self-deferring thesis. MARGIN uses it as proof the supplier-margin bear case is dead and promotes VRT back to #1. One print cannot be evidence of scarcity, a cheap entry, and a margin all-clear simultaneously without someone double-counting.
  • MEM'S TOP PICK NO LONGER EXISTS AT THE PRICE IT WAS UNDERWRITTEN. MEM named Samsung 005930.KS at 216,500 won (2026-07-30 close) as the cheapest name in the group at ~10x forward and ~41% below its H1 peak. Verified 2026-08-03 Korea close: 239,500 won — +10.6% ABOVE the cited entry, and now ~36% rather than ~41% below its 374,500 high, despite closing -8.76% that very session. Worse, the Korean line fell 8.76% on the same date the US complex rose 5-14%, so the node's cross-market relative-value argument inverted inside the read window.
  • DEMAND-SIDE CRACKS ARE APPEARING EXACTLY WHERE THE SYSTEM CLAIMS TIGHTNESS 9, AND THREE ARE INDEPENDENT. Exelon cut its high-probability data-center load ~40% to 11 GW and its total pipeline ~16% to ~36 GW on 2026-07-30 after screening speculative requests through transmission service agreements; nVent's backlog declined sequentially to $2.5B from $2.6B with organic orders decelerating from ~40% to low-double-digit on 2026-07-31, the cohort's first this cycle; and spot DDR5 median fell 4.4% w/w to $31.75/GB against contract RDIMM +80-120% QoQ. This dashboard treats backlog as the leading indicator and price as lagging. If a meaningful share of that backlog is phantom load being pruned, then backlog is measuring INTENT, not demand, and the core premise of the framework inverts.
  • CPU'S OWN EVIDENCE CUTS AGAINST ITS OWN SCORE. The node holds tightness at 9 and retains INTC as a pick, while presenting IDC data (2026-07-21, restated by Arm 2026-07-29) showing Arm accelerated-server spend at $53.0B versus x86 at $34.6B, with x86 accelerated value HALVING from ~$52B in just two quarters. A shortage whose addressable base inside the AI buildout is collapsing at that rate cannot support a 9 for long, and the node says so itself before scoring it anyway. The same tension appears in ACCEL, which cut tightness to 8 because HBM4 relieved, yet kept the node at peak_tightness.

Prediction model & backtest gate

This is the investability guardrail. The free model tests whether filing-known fundamental acceleration, under-coverage, and not-priced price position have historically predicted 13-week excess return. It is a triage model unless the clustered backtest passes and a point-in-time consensus/valuation feed proves the Street's forward numbers are wrong.

Unified alpha shortlist — with an early-entry action gate

The research picks and the computed screen merged into ONE list, each routed through an action gate: EARLY-ENTRY (requires not-priced acceleration and a quantified expectations/valuation gap) · WATCH-TRIGGER (right node, needs a catalyst or consensus proof) · PRICED-MOMENTUM (already run) · AVOID / MARGIN-RISK (margin, inventory, or financing stress — NOT alpha). EE = early-entry score (0–10), but no ticker is action-grade while the consensus feed is missing, current-snapshot-only, or non-vintage. Each row carries the thesis for why it may not be fully priced, an honest AI-purity est., and a falsifier. The valuation/consensus gap is the one open data feed (needs FMP or verified manual PIT estimates) — until then this is a triage queue, not a buy list. With ^SOX +101%/6mo, "not-priced" means relatively un-run, not cheap.

Research-layer synthesis — reconciled with the action gate:

No research thesis overrides the gate. If a name is priced, it is a thesis to monitor, not an early entry.

Actionable early entries: none until the expectations/valuation gate is wired.

Watch triggers

  • HUBB [GRID (utility T&D / substation componentry — the binding constraint's unpriced leg)] WATCH · research — The market believes Hubbell is a low-beta electrical-equipment industrial with a lagging utility-distribution and telecom-enclosure business, and has priced it as such: beta 0.892, +7.11% YTD, -5.59% over SIX MONTHS, trading $476.13 below both its 50-day ($489.60) and 200-day ($483.17). The evidence says it is a direct owner of the sub-link that CLOUD, COOL, SERVER and MARGIN all independently defer to — its Utility Solutions segment designs and sells electrical distribution, transmission and SUBSTATION products, the literal iron between an interconnection approval and an energized megawatt. This is the only US utility-T&D franchise in the node that the AI-grid multiple has never been applied to, and it is a multiple-convergence call, not an orders-acceleration call.
  • AMKR [PKG / ACCEL / NETOPT (the shared 2.5D/3D advanced-packaging queue)] WATCH · research — The market believes Amkor is a low-margin commodity OSAT caught in an AI-capex unwind and has marked it down ~48% from its high on a sector sentiment shock, to below its 200-day. The evidence says three separate nodes independently rotated their binding sub-link into the advanced-packaging queue Amkor owns capacity in — PKG on tool-install and building-completion throughput, ACCEL on CoWoS-L and hybrid bonding, and NETOPT on TrendForce's finding that co-packaged optics now competes directly with AI chips for the same 2.5D/3D packaging capacity. Nothing in the packaging chain confirmed the bear case: no order cuts, no pushouts, no lead-time compression were reported anywhere inside the window, while Amkor's own numbers went up.
  • IREN [CLOUD / GRID (powered capacity owned outright, funded by customer prepayment)] WATCH · research — The market still classifies IREN as a Bitcoin miner — the data vendor's own live profile describes its primary undertaking as mining Bitcoin — and prices it inside a neocloud complex it is uniformly de-rating for funding and dilution risk. The evidence says the funding constraint about to bind the sector is already solved on its balance sheet: roughly 45% of related GPU capex is PREPAID by customers, which structurally removes the dilution bear case that is the entire short thesis on neoclouds. If the binding constraint of the whole system is energized deliverable megawatts, the economics must transfer to whoever already holds contracted, powered capacity — and prepayment-backed revenue is a categorically different asset from raw RPO, a distinction the tape is not yet making.

DEMAND — Is end-demand accelerating ahead of expectations? (capex, cloud AI revenue, backlog/RPO, GPU availability)

A · Hyperscaler & Neocloud Capex / Cloud AI DemandConfirmed shortage
tightness 9/10priced 6/10bullconf: medium

The biggest earnings week of the year landed inside this window and it broke decisively toward "shortage worse than modeled." Amazon's contracted cloud backlog jumped to $496B, up $132B in a single quarter, AWS grew 36.7% (fastest in 18 quarters), 2026 capex was raised $200B to $220B, and Jassy said outright that Amazon will not have enough capacity to meet demand in 2026 and that "this dynamic will also be true in 2027" — with most 2027 capacity already reserved and a significant part of 2028 committed. Microsoft matched it: Azure +43% with Q1 guided to ~45% cc (accelerating, not decelerating), 31 datacenters and "another gigawatt" added in one quarter, and Amy Hood repeating that demand continues to exceed available capacity with new capacity "immediately monetized." The single most useful number of the week came from Applied Digital's 10-K: 1,410 MW contracted across five campuses against roughly 100 MW actually energized — a 7% conversion rate that quantifies exactly where the bottleneck now sits. Equity reaction split hard: the neocloud complex ripped ~30% on 7/30, but that was a bounce off a wrecked base, not a re-rating — IREN is still only +3% YTD and 49% below its 52-week high, CRWV +12% and 47% below, while NBIS at +149% YTD has clearly left the early zone. The honest cross-current is Meta: EPS missed ~14%, FCF collapsed to $784M and the stock fell ~9.6%, so the market is now openly punishing capex without near-term revenue — which is a risk to multiples, not to physical tightness. Note also that Microsoft's calendar-2026 capex optically stepped down toward ~$175B, but that reflects useful-life and lease reclassification accounting rather than reduced buildout intent.

  • Amazon Q2 2026: AWS revenue $42.2B, +36.7% YoY (fastest growth in 18 quarters), AWS operating income $16.6B at 39.4% margin; contracted cloud backlog reached $496B, up $132B sequentially in one quarter; 2026 cash capex guidance raised from ~$200B to ~$220B. Andy Jassy: Amazon will not "have enough capacity to meet all the demand we have in 2026. And I believe this dynamic will also be true in 2027, too," adding that most 2027 capacity is already reserved and a significant portion of 2028 has commitments, and that AWS remains on pace to double power capacity by end-2027 vs 2025. — Amazon Q2 2026 earnings release and call (CNBC / Fortune / Digitimes / TechTimes coverage) · 2026-07-30
  • Microsoft FQ4 FY2026: revenue ~$90B (+18%), Microsoft Cloud $59.3B (+27%), Azure and other cloud services +43% with Azure passing $100B annual revenue for the first time; quarterly capex $41B (about two-thirds short-lived CPU/GPU assets); Q1 FY2027 capex guided above $50B and FY2027 capex to grow further on "demand signals across our portfolio." Satya Nadella said Microsoft added 31 datacenters across five continents and "another gigawatt of capacity" in the quarter; Amy Hood said "demand continued to exceed available capacity" and that added capacity is "immediately monetized," guiding Q1 Azure growth to ~45% in constant currency — an acceleration from 43%. — Microsoft FY26 Q4 earnings release and conference call (Microsoft IR; CNBC, Benzinga, Directions on Microsoft coverage) · 2026-07-29
  • Applied Digital FY2026 10-K: approximately $36.2B of total contracted lease backlog over initial 15-year base terms, across 1,410 MW of critical IT load at five campuses (Polaris Forge 1 400MW/CoreWeave, Polaris Forge 2 200MW, Polaris Forge 3 300MW, Delta Forge 1 300MW, Delta Forge 2 210MW) — but only approximately 100 MW energized and revenue-generating as of 2026-05-31. Fiscal Q4 (reported 2026-07-27) revenue was $258.7M versus roughly $95.3M consensus, adjusted EPS $0.04 versus a $0.19 expected loss; ~$20B of the backlog was signed in the last quarter alone; financing secured via $2.15B senior secured notes plus a $300M bridge facility. — Applied Digital Corporation FY2026 Form 10-K and fiscal Q4 earnings release (SEC / StockTitan / Investing.com) · 2026-07-29
  • Neocloud equities rallied roughly 30% on 2026-07-30 on the back of the hyperscaler cloud prints plus a WSJ report that Citadel bought most of hedge fund Situational Awareness's public equity book (which had disclosed a $2.6B NBIS stake in May). Despite the pop, as of 2026-08-03 the group remains far below prior highs: IREN $38.93 (+3.06% YTD, 52-week high $76.87, low $15.49), CRWV $80.54 (+12.47% YTD, 52-week high $153.20, low $60.55), APLD $29.21 (+19.13% YTD, 52-week high $50.72) — while NBIS $208.69 is +149.32% YTD against a 52-week high of $299.86. — IBKR read-only price snapshots (NASDAQ) taken 2026-08-03; TipRanks / Stocktwits coverage of the 2026-07-30 move · 2026-08-03
  • Meta Q2 2026: revenue $60.8B (+28%, a beat), but diluted EPS $6.18 missed consensus of ~$7.22 by roughly 14%, capex surged to $31.1B in the quarter, free cash flow collapsed to $784M, and full-year 2026 capex guidance was set at $130-145B. Shares fell about 9.6% after hours as investors pressed on ROI for infrastructure spend — the clearest evidence this week that the market is now penalizing the capex-to-revenue gap even while physical capacity stays short. — Meta Platforms Q2 2026 earnings release (Seeking Alpha / TradingKey / Digital Applied coverage) · 2026-07-29

Rotation: The binding sub-link stayed exactly where it was last week — ENERGIZED, NEAR-TERM-DELIVERABLE MEGAWATTS — but this week it got quantified rather than merely asserted, which is the meaningful change. Applied Digital's 10-K puts 1,410 MW under contract against roughly 100 MW energized as of 2026-05-31: a 7% conversion rate. Demand is signed; electrons are not flowing. The supporting industry data is consistent: interconnection queues of four to seven years in Northern Virginia, Phoenix and Dallas, ERCOT's large-load queue at roughly 226 GW, large power transformer lead times stretched to 36-48 months, and only about one-third of the ~12 GW of US AI datacenter capacity announced for 2026 actually under active construction. Jassy's statement that AWS must double its power capacity by end-2027 and still expects to be short in both 2026 and 2027 is the demand-side confirmation from the largest builder on earth. The constraint continues rotating one step further into FUNDING and DELIVERY capacity — who can finance and energize GPUs without dilution — which is why prepayment-backed contracts (IREN, ~45% of GPU capex prepaid) and investment-grade take-or-pay leases (APLD's five campuses, CoreWeave's $8.5B IG-rated HPC-backed facility) are structurally higher-quality backlog than raw RPO. Upstream, real physical constraint owners include Taiwan-listed names (TSMC 2330.TW for CoWoS, Quanta 2382.TW and Wistron 3231.TW for rack integration) — per mandate these are CONTEXT ONLY and are never picks; the tradable expression is routed to US-listed powered-capacity owners instead. No rotation of the bottleneck this week; it hardened.

Leading metric: Backlog-to-capex coverage: sequential change in contracted cloud backlog/RPO divided by sequential capex. This quarter it blew out — AWS alone added $132B of backlog against roughly $50-55B of quarterly capex (coverage >2x), on top of Alphabet's +$50B QoQ to $514B last week. Threshold that would flip the read: aggregate ΔRPO/Δcapex falling below 1.0 for two consecutive quarters, i.e. the buildout finally out-running new bookings. Fastest secondary tell, unchanged: the energization conversion rate — contracted MW versus actually energized MW at the powered-capacity owners (APLD is at 100/1,410 = 7%). A jump in that ratio toward 25-30% without a corresponding jump in new contracted MW is the first true easing signal. Tertiary: neocloud contract pricing per GPU-hour rolling over, and any hyperscaler cancelling a third-party capacity bridge.

SUPPLY TIGHTNESS — Where is qualified supply lagging demand? (the binding constraint — do not merge bottlenecks too early; they rotate)

C · Accelerators — GPU / Custom ASICPeak tightness
tightness 8/10priced 6/10neutralconf: medium

The accelerator node's physical scarcity is intact but its price is not: the SOX fell 21% in July — its worst month since October 2008 — erasing $2.2T and leaving the index 23% below its 22-Jun record with every constituent red, while nothing in the supply data actually broke. That de-rate is severe enough that NVDA (last $205.96, +10.4% YTD, 13% below its 52-wk high of $236.54) and AVGO ($382.75, +10.8% YTD, 23% below its $495 high) are no longer expensive against their own 2026 fundamentals, even as AMD (+123% YTD) and MRVL (+122% YTD) remain the crowded half of the node. The one genuine supply change this week ran against the scarcity story: the HBM4 16-Hi link that last week's read named as the rotating bottleneck resolved early and favorably — SK hynix confirmed HBM4 mass shipments already began in Q2 (29 Jul) and Samsung guided HBM4 revenue up more than 3x QoQ in Q3 and above 60% of HBM sales in 2H (30 Jul), with qualification "progressing smoothly" and HBM4E samples already out. Demand did not crack in the same window: Meta lifted FY26 capex to $130-145B, AWS net sales grew 37% YoY, Alphabet had already raised FY26 capex to $195-205B on 22 Jul, and Samsung explicitly said the 2027 orders already on its books imply a wider supply-demand gap than 2026. Advantest independently raised its CY26 tester TAM ~19% to $13.0-14.5B on 29 Jul, which is a hard, dated upgrade to accelerator unit assumptions, not a downgrade. So far this is a multiple event, not a fundamental one. Net: tightness eases one notch to 8 because the specific named sub-link relieved, priced falls hard from 8 to 6, and for the first time this year the early-vs-priced gap in ACCEL is widening rather than closing. The honest caveat is that retail poured a record $12B into semiconductor ETFs in the final week of July alone — the crowd is buying this dip, positioning is not washed out, and a falling tape with a live capex-to-revenue-gap narrative behind it does not earn a bull tag yet.

  • SK hynix reported 2Q26 revenue of KRW 79.3187T, operating profit of KRW 60.5426T and a 76% operating margin, and stated it "began mass shipments of HBM4 in the second quarter and will ramp up production in the second half of the year" — earlier than the Q3-26 volume start assumed in the prior week's read, materially relieving the HBM4 16-Hi qualification bottleneck. — SK hynix official newsroom, 2Q26 business results release · 2026-07-29
  • Samsung Electronics reported record Q2-26 operating profit of KRW 89.49T (+1,813.8% YoY), said HBM4 customer qualification is progressing smoothly with Q3 HBM4 revenue to rise more than 3x QoQ and HBM4 to exceed 60% of total HBM revenue in 2H, shipped the industry's first HBM4E samples, and warned that "judging solely from the orders already received for 2027, the supply-demand gap will widen further compared to 2026." — Samsung Electronics Q2 2026 results / earnings call coverage (Samsung Global Newsroom, Seoul Economic Daily, TradingKey) · 2026-07-30
  • Advantest reported record Q1 FY26 sales of JPY 367.5B (+39.3% YoY) and operating income of JPY 190.0B (+53.3% YoY), raised full-year FY26 guidance, and lifted its CY2026 tester TAM forecast to $13.0-14.5B from the April forecast of $10.9-12.2B (~+19%), citing higher-than-expected AI semiconductor production volumes and rising device complexity in advanced-packaging AI silicon. — Advantest Q1 FY26 results presentation and earnings call (Investing.com slides/transcript coverage) · 2026-07-29
  • The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell 21% in July 2026 — its worst month since October 2008 — erasing $2.2T of market capitalisation and leaving the index 23% below its 22-Jun-2026 record high with every constituent in the red; TSMC fell 15% (-$380B), Micron 29% (-$374B), Intel 35% (-$247B). Retail investors simultaneously put a record $12B into semiconductor-focused ETFs in the final week of July, ~25% of all ETF inflows. — Fortune, "Wall Street's favorite bet comes undone as chips whipsaw market" · 2026-08-02
  • Hyperscaler capex was raised, not cut, inside the same window: Meta guided FY26 capex to $130-145B (low end up $5B) with Q2 capex of $31.1B on 29 Jul; Amazon reported Q2 net sales of $200.6B with AWS at $42.2B, +37% YoY — its fastest growth in 18 quarters — on 30 Jul; Alphabet had raised FY26 capex to $195-205B from $180-190B on 22 Jul. No Big Tech reporter attributed spending delays to accelerator shortage; only Apple cited advanced chip-packaging capacity constraints as a cost pressure. — Company Q2-2026 results and capex guidance, compiled in DigitalApplied AI Capex Scorecard; CNBC hyperscaler capex coverage · 2026-07-30

Rotation: The binding sub-link rotated this week — and it rotated backwards. Last week's read named HBM4 16-Hi stack qualification and yield as the newly binding constraint (SK hynix volume slipping to Q3-26, 8 stacks / 288GB per Rubin GPU). That link measurably relieved inside this window: SK hynix confirmed HBM4 mass shipments had already started in Q2 with yields near HBM3E maturity (29 Jul), and Samsung guided HBM4 up >3x QoQ in Q3 to >60% of HBM revenue in 2H with qualification "progressing smoothly" and HBM4E samples shipped (30 Jul). With three qualified HBM4 sources ramping, the ceiling reverts to (a) CoWoS-L slot count — still sold out through 2026 with 52-78 week lead times, and TrendForce's 15-Jun read that the supply-demand gap narrows from ~20% to ~10% by end-2026 as TSMC scales from ~75k to 125-130k wpm — and (b) final test, where Advantest's 19% TAM raise on 29 Jul is the most direct in-window confirmation that test seconds per accelerator are inflating faster than unit counts. Ownership note: the packaging constraint owner is TSMC (2330.TW) with design-service overflow at Alchip (3661.TW) and Global Unichip (3443.TW) — all Taiwan-listed and therefore CONTEXT ONLY, never pickable here; exposure is routed to the JP test link (6857.T), the EU hybrid-bonding link (BESI.AS) and the US second-source custom-ASIC vendor (MRVL). The genuinely new candidate bottleneck to watch is financial rather than physical: CreditSights documents that aggregate big-five capex now exceeds cash flow after buybacks and dividends, so 2027 accelerator builds are increasingly debt-funded — that, not silicon, is the most plausible thing that breaks first.

Leading metric: TSMC monthly revenue YoY growth (self-reported on the 10th of each month; next print 2026-08-10 for July), read against Advantest's newly raised CY26 tester TAM of $13.0-14.5B. This is now the cleanest single gauge because it aggregates CoWoS throughput, N3/N2 accelerator wafer starts and HBM base-die logic in one dated number that no longer depends on the HBM4 story. Flip thresholds: two consecutive monthly prints below +25% YoY, or Advantest cutting the CY26 TAM at its October quarter, would confirm the July equity de-rate is discounting real accelerator order cuts and flip this node bear. Conversely, monthly prints holding at or above +30% YoY through the September release with the tester TAM intact would prove the July move was pure multiple compression against unchanged physical demand, and flips the node bull with priced still near 6.

D · Host CPUPeak tightness
tightness 9/10priced 5/10neutralconf: medium

Nothing loosened physically this week, but the equities in this node were repriced violently. Server-CPU channel conditions are unchanged at their extreme — ~40% Intel allocation fill, EPYC sold out through year-end, 30+ week lead times, Xeon SKUs already >$1,000 above 2025 list with a further 8-10% hike signaled for 2H26 — while Intel's Q2 (7/23) DCAI +59% and a capex raise from $18B to >$20B confirmed no catch-up before 2027. What changed is the price: an AI-capex-sustainability panic on 7/29 stripped >$1T of chip market cap (Nvidia alone -$238B) after Alphabet lifted 2026 capex to $195-205B on 7/28, and every name in this node fell hard over the past month — INTC -26.6%, ARM -27.8%, AMD -13.4%, Ibiden -33.5%, Ajinomoto -20.6% — with none of that driven by CPU supply improving. That is a genuine improvement in entry, not in thesis, so priced comes down 6→5 while tightness stays 9. The second real change is structural and bearish for merchant x86 specifically: IDC data (published 7/21, restated by Arm in its 7/29 release) shows Arm/non-x86 accelerated-server spend at $53.0B in Q1'26 versus x86 at $34.6B, with x86 accelerated value collapsing from ~$52B to $34.6B in just two quarters as buyers consolidated onto NVDA rack-scale Arm platforms. Arm's own print the same day was strong — $1.29B revenue +22%, data-center royalties more than doubled, Neoverse past 1.5B cumulative cores with the last 500M in nine months, and its own 136-core AGI server CPU carrying >$2B of demand across FYE27-28. Read: head-node scarcity is real but is migrating out of the merchant x86 socket and into bundled Arm silicon, so the durable un-priced exposure still sits one layer down in the back-end substrate that gates every one of them. The one honest crack to watch is demand, not supply — spot DDR5 median ticked down 4.4% w/w even as contract RDIMM printed +80-120% QoQ, which is exactly how these cycles start to roll.

Rotation: The physical binding sub-link is UNCHANGED from last week and still not the CPU die: Intel's CFO named back-end ABF/FC-BGA package substrate and server DRAM on 2026-07-23, and neither eased. High-end FC-BGA lead times sit at 20-28 weeks against ~14% annual demand growth; the pool is Ibiden ~35%, Shinko ~18% (delisted, not investable), Unimicron ~14%, AT&S ~10%, Nan Ya PCB ~5%, with Ajinomoto's ABF dielectric film a de-facto monopoly upstream of all of them and an HPC substrate consuming >10x the film of a PC substrate. What DID rotate this week is one layer up, on the demand-composition side rather than the capacity side: IDC's Q1'26 data (7/21, restated by Arm 7/29) shows accelerated-rack head nodes are now majority Arm — $53.0B versus $34.6B x86, with x86 accelerated value halving in two quarters — so merchant x86 head-node scarcity is concentrating into general-purpose, RL-training and CPU-inference fleets rather than AI-rack head nodes, which increasingly ship as bundled NVDA Vera/Grace silicon. Practical consequence: the x86 shortage is still real and still repricing, but its addressable base inside the AI buildout is shrinking, which caps how much of this node's tightness can accrue to INTC and AMD versus to the substrate layer that gates every architecture equally. Routing note unchanged: two of the five FC-BGA owners (Unimicron 3037.TW, Nan Ya PCB 8046.TW) are Taiwan-listed and are CONTEXT ONLY per mandate — excluded from picks and routed to the Japanese equivalents.

Leading metric: Primary: high-end FC-BGA (ABF) substrate lead time, currently 20-28 weeks with 6-month+ quotes on high-layer-count variants. FLIP THRESHOLD: a print below ~20 weeks, or Ibiden guiding its Electronics segment to flat-or-down sequential revenue at its Q1 FY2026 (Apr-Jun) release due ~2026-08-05, is the rollover — CPU shipment tightness follows within ~2 quarters and the CPU-lead-time headlines will miss it. Secondary confirm: distributor fill rate on 64-core-and-above x86 SKUs rising above ~60% from today's ~40%. NEW third gauge added this week: spot-versus-contract DDR5 spread — spot median fell 4.4% w/w to $31.75/GB while contract RDIMM printed +80-120% QoQ; two more consecutive down weeks in spot with contract still rising is the classic first crack, and it would flip this node from peak_tightness toward easing ahead of any CPU-side datapoint.

E · Memory — HBM / DRAM / NANDPeak tightness
tightness 9/10priced 8/10bearconf: medium

Three record prints in five days, and the node's equities fell on two of them — that is the whole story of this week. SK hynix (2026-07-29) posted 79.32tn won revenue (+257% YoY) and 60.54tn won operating profit (+557% YoY, 76% margin), yet missed LSEG SmartEstimates of ~84tn/64tn and fell 8.98%, while simultaneously raising 2026 capex ~50% to the "high 40tn won range" (~45tn won / ~$31bn) to pull in M15X and Yongin Phase-1 — the first hard supply-response datapoint of this cycle, delivered by the cycle's own leader. Seagate (2026-07-28) beat on every line (revenue $3.63bn +49% YoY, non-GAAP GM 52.7% vs 37.9%, EPS $5.71 vs $5.10 consensus) and said most nearline exabytes are already allocated into calendar 2028 — and the stock still traded -4.65% at $816.28 on 2026-08-03. The lone exception was Samsung (2026-07-30): operating profit 89.49tn won (+1,813.8% YoY), industry-first HBM4E samples shipped, HBM4 revenue guided to more than triple QoQ in 3Q, and an explicit statement that orders already booked for 2027 imply a WIDER supply-demand gap than 2026 — and it closed +3.72% at 216,500 won. So physical tightness did not ease this week; it was re-confirmed out to 2028. What changed is that the market stopped paying for tightness and started pricing the supply response: SK hynix's capex, ChangXin Memory's newly funded Shanghai STAR listing, and a contract-price second derivative that has collapsed from +93-98% QoQ (1Q26) to +55-63% (2Q26) to +13-18% (3Q26). Every leaf now sits 28-47% below its 52-week high while still up 130-425% YTD — a broken parabola, not a discount, and the priced score should not be softened by the drawdown. Tightness holds at 9 and priced holds at 8; the signal flips neutral-to-bear, because when three record quarters buy you one green close out of three, the informational edge in this node is spent.

  • Contract-price momentum has decelerated sharply while HBM 2027 pricing is unresolved. Rotation: The binding sub-link rotated again this week — and for the first time it rotated out of physics and into capital. On units the tightest links remain (a) HBM4 wafer plus TSV/advanced-packaging attach, (b) high-capacity server RDIMM, where lead times run 20-40+ weeks against a normal 8-12 and Tier-1 cloud order books are filling at roughly 70%, and (c) nearline HDD, which visibly TIGHTENED in-window — Seagate said on 7/28 that most nearline exabytes are already allocated into calendar 2028, with HAMR/Mozaic at 40% of nearline exabyte shipments. Within HBM the constraint has migrated from "can they build it" to "what does 2027 price at": all three suppliers were qualified on HBM4 for Vera Rubin back on 2026-06-05, so the chokepoint is now the stalled 2027 contract negotiation (suppliers pushing ~$2/Gb in 2H26 toward $4-5/Gb), not yield. But the constraint that now binds the EQUITY is neither of those — it is the capital cycle. SK hynix's own ~50% capex raise to ~45tn won and CXMT's ~$4.1bn STAR-market war chest are the first two credible supply-response datapoints of this cycle, and they arrive years before any of the tightness resolves (Yongin Phase-1 cleanroom early 2027, Samsung P5 2028). Carried forward from last week and unchanged: SK hynix's HBM4 base die is fabbed by TSMC (2330.TW), which stays CONTEXT-ONLY under the no-Taiwan mandate, with tradable routing to the KR/US principals. One structural asymmetry worth isolating: nearline HDD is the single sub-link where the CXMT/China bear case cannot apply — China has no HDD head or media capability — which is exactly why STX and WDC should be scored separately from the DRAM/HBM tightness read rather than folded into it.

Leading metric: TrendForce's 4Q26 server-DRAM contract-price forecast, read as a SECOND derivative rather than a level. The observed sequence is +93-98% QoQ (1Q26) → +55-63% (2Q26) → +13-18% (3Q26). A 4Q26 guide of 0-5% QoQ or lower confirms the pricing cycle has rolled over and takes this node to `easing` regardless of how sold-out the order books read; a 4Q26 guide back above roughly +20% QoQ would invalidate this week's bear flip and argue the 3Q deceleration was a consumer-mix and base-effect artifact rather than a turn. Confirming gauge: server-grade DDR5 RDIMM SPOT going to a sustained DISCOUNT to contract (it still carries a premium today), plus supplier finished-goods DRAM inventory weeks turning up two months running from near zero.

Distributor lead-time (live, computed): MEM-BOOT-NOR 44wk · 1/4 parts tight (DigiKey+Mouser) — the hard 2nd source: a real shortage shows up here before it prints in revenue.

F · Advanced Packaging & SubstratesConfirmed shortage
tightness 8/10priced 5/10bullconf: medium

This was the week the PKG node's fundamentals and its share prices went in opposite directions, which is the single most important thing that has happened to this node since the dashboard started tracking it. Every in-window print got BETTER: Amkor posted a record Q2 ($1.90bn, +25.6% y/y, GM 16.8% vs 12.0%) and guided Q3 UP to $1.95-2.05bn with GM 18.5-19.5%; ASE raised 2026 capex a second time from US$8.5bn to a record US$10.5bn, said 2027 LEAP revenue will DOUBLE, and stated plainly that "demand is not the concern" — growth is gated by how fast tools can be installed and buildings finished; ASMPT printed a 1.43 book-to-bill, its highest since 1H-2021, on H1 bookings +85.1% y/y and took a July order for 50+ chip-to-substrate TCB tools. Meanwhile the tape collapsed: SK hynix's Q2 miss on 2026-07-29 triggered a >US$1trn drawdown across chip stocks, and the PKG complex de-rated 30-50% from its 52-week highs — AMKR at $49.77 is 48% below its $96.68 high and below its 200-day; Besi at EUR198.8 is 39% off its high and sitting exactly on its 200-day; Camtek is below its 200-day; Ibiden is 43% off. Nothing in the packaging supply chain confirmed the bear case: no order cuts, no pushouts, no lead-time compression. The binding sub-link also rotated again — from ABF film alone to film PLUS physical build-out throughput (equipment installation, cleanroom completion) and now upstream CCL/high-end PCB materials. So the honest read flips from "tight but getting spent" to "still tight, and the market just handed back a third of the price": tightness stays 8, priced drops 7 to 5, and the best exposure moves from the Japanese upstream to the cheapest US-listed constraint owner. The one thing that would invalidate this is a genuine AI-capex break showing up in Q4 bookings rather than in sentiment.

Rotation: The binding sub-link rotated AGAIN this week, and in a direction that argues against easing. Prior read had it at ABF build-up film (Ajinomoto ~95% share, ~30% price hike live from Q3-2026, i.e. now). That is still binding — Unimicron's CFO confirmed on 2026-07-30 that raw-material costs have risen and are being passed through — but two new sub-links surfaced in-window. (1) PHYSICAL BUILD-OUT THROUGHPUT: ASE stated on 2026-07-30 that growth is gated by how fast equipment can be installed and buildings completed, not by demand, even at only 80-85% blended utilization; that is a construction/tool-install constraint, and it is why ASE added US$2bn of capex rather than raising the revenue guide alone. (2) UPSTREAM LAMINATE: DigiTimes on 2026-08-03 ('AI infrastructure orders extend PCB expansion race') flags upstream CCL/copper-clad laminate as already short, one layer below the ABF film itself, and on 2026-07-31 ('AI strains mature-node and packaging capacity, leaving chip designers with orders but no supply') reports backend packaging providers raising prices as constraints spread. CoWoS/interposer remains sold out but is NOT the marginal constraint — TSMC capacity is compounding toward 125-130k wpm by end-2026 and ~170-200k wpm in 2027, and the gap narrows from ~20% to ~10%. The genuine fabrication constraint owners remain Taiwan-listed (Unimicron 3037.TW, Nan Ya PCB 8046.TW, Kinsus 3189.TW) and are CONTEXT ONLY here, so exposure is routed to US OSAT, JP upstream material and EU/JP tooling. Ibiden 4062.T is the purest listed FC-BGA constraint owner but reports Q1 FY2026 on 2026-08-04 — one day after this as-of date — and fell 6.7% on 2026-08-03 into the print, so it is deliberately held out of the pick list this week rather than taken as a coin-flip.

Leading metric: Advanced-packaging TOOL book-to-bill read against ABF/FC-BGA finished-substrate lead time. Right now both point the same way: ASMPT 1.43 book-to-bill (H1-26, best since 1H-2021), Besi record Q2 bookings EUR292.9m, and substrate lead times still 16-24 weeks finished / 20-30 weeks quoted in Taiwan versus a 12-16 week norm. The threshold that flips the read: ASMPT or Besi printing a book-to-bill below 1.0 for a full quarter WHILE lead times compress back under ~16 weeks — that pair, not a single soft guide and not a share-price drawdown, marks confirmed_shortage turning to easing. Conversely, if lead times hold above 20 weeks through Q4-2026 while tool bookings keep rising, then the July 28-29 de-rating was a price event and the priced score should be cut again rather than the tightness score.

G · Passive Components — MLCC & InductorsConfirmed shortage
tightness 9/10priced 6/10bullconf: medium

This was the week the physical data and the share prices went in opposite directions, which is the whole reason this node matters again. Murata's 2026-07-31 Q1 print pushed the capacitor book-to-bill from 1.36 to 1.47, with capacitor orders +85.5% y/y to JPY 415.5bn, backlog +170.5% y/y to JPY 402.2bn, ~95% utilization, and FY guidance lifted by JPY 150bn revenue / JPY 50bn OP to record levels. Samsung Electro-Mechanics printed Q2 OP +107% y/y on 2026-07-30, disclosed MLCC long-term agreements with roughly ten global customers including hyperscalers, and notified partners of a blanket 30% MLCC price increase for shipments on or after 2026-08-01; Taiyo Yuden notified customers on 2026-07-23 of its own adjustment from 2026-09-01. TDK and Kyocera both printed records on 2026-07-30/31. Against all that, the equities were crushed: over the past month Taiyo Yuden -51.8%, Murata -35.4%, Vishay -23.9%, with Murata -44% and Taiyo Yuden -59% from their 52-week highs, driven mostly by the broad AI/semis de-rating (Nasdaq 100 approaching correction, SOX ~-20% off its June peak) rather than by anything in the passives data. So tightness stays pinned at 9 while priced drops 8 to 6 — the gap that defines alpha widened this week rather than closed. The one genuine crack to respect: Murata management said long-term-customer orders were broadly in line with plan while distributor orders ran well ahead, which is textbook pre-hike inventory building and is exactly how the 2018 cycle topped; Yageo's Q2 book-to-bill of 2.2 (from 1.5) alongside ~4 months of global distributor inventory says the same thing louder. Last week's picks are corrected below: Kyocera delivered (OP +164.7%, FY net guide raised to JPY 160bn) and has largely closed its discount at +59% YTD and only -12% off its high, and my claim that TDK was "flat-to-negative YTD" was simply wrong — it is +37% YTD.

Rotation: The binding sub-link stayed upstream but its expression rotated this week from lead-time to price and allocation. Physical capacity is unchanged and still capped: Murata at ~95% utilization with capex only nudged to JPY 255bn and roughly 20% capacity growth over two years against AI MLCC demand growing far faster, and industry channel inventory under 30 days. What changed is that suppliers stopped rationing by queue and started rationing by contract and price — SEMCO's blanket 30% increase effective 2026-08-01, Taiyo Yuden's notice for 2026-09-01, Yageo's ~50% July increase extended to direct customers with management explicitly not ruling out more, and SEMCO's ~KRW 2.3tn of cumulative long-term agreements now covering roughly ten global customers including hyperscalers (LTAs are rare in a market that normally runs on short-term orders). Underneath finished MLCC the constraint set is unchanged: hydrothermal fine barium titanate dielectric powder (Sakai Chemical 4078.T, Nippon Chemical Industrial, Fuji Titanium) and sub-100nm nickel electrode powder (Toho Titanium 5727.T, Sumitomo Metal Mining, unlisted Shoei Chemical), plus green-sheet stacking equipment on ~1.5-year lead times. A second, less-crowded sub-link inside this node is the TLVR / power-inductor lane, where high-frequency and trans-inductor parts sit with a small set of Japanese and European makers and are far less written about than MLCC — TDK is #1 there at 45-50% share. The genuine constraint owners remain Japan and Korea, not Taiwan: Yageo (2327.TW) and Tai-Tech (3357.TWO) capture overflow and pricing spillover — Yageo's Q2 book-to-bill of 2.2 versus 1.5 in Q1 is the loudest spillover signal in the node — but they do not own the high-end chokepoint, so the tradable expression routes to the JP majors and to US-listed Vishay, not to Taipei.

Leading metric: Murata's capacitor book-to-bill, now 1.47 (up from 1.36 last quarter, versus the 1.25 orders-to-backlog print that marked the 2018 shortage onset) — but read it split, because Murata itself disclosed on 2026-07-31 that direct long-term-customer orders were only in line with plan while distributor orders drove the beat. The read flips bearish on any ONE of: (a) capacitor B/B printing below ~1.10 for two consecutive quarters; (b) direct-customer order growth decelerating below shipment growth while distributor orders keep climbing for another quarter, i.e. confirmed double-ordering; or (c) Samsung Electro-Mechanics' 2026-08-01 30% hike or Taiyo Yuden's 2026-09-01 hike being discounted back or quietly not enforced. It stays bullish if the 30% hike sticks into Q3 shipments and Murata's autumn direct-customer price negotiations land. Next hard prints: Vishay Q2 and Taiyo Yuden Q1 both on 2026-08-05, Murata Q2 late October.

Distributor lead-time (live, computed): PAS-POLYCAP 28wk · 7/19 parts tight (DigiKey+Mouser) — the hard 2nd source: a real shortage shows up here before it prints in revenue.

H · Power Silicon — VRM / Power-Stage / GaN-SiCPeak tightness
tightness 9/10priced 7/10bullconf: medium

The physical read got tighter this week while the equity read got cheaper — the first genuine early-vs-priced gap this node has offered since April. Four in-window prints all said the same thing: Monolithic Power (7/30) posted a record $980.6M quarter with Enterprise Data +164% yoy and raised its FY26 enterprise-data growth floor from 85% to 130% with book-to-bill "well above 1.0x" and channel inventory "very low"; Renesas (7/31) called data-center demand "exceptionally strong" with tight supply persisting and channel inventory down to under 8 weeks from 12-14; Littelfuse (7/29) printed record bookings and guided Q3 to ~+26% yoy; and Delta Electronics (7/30, Taiwan, context-only) — the largest merchant rack-PSU maker — said memory and MOSFETs are "already fully constrained" with procurement lead times lengthening into 2H. Against that, the node's equities de-rated hard through July on AI-capex and AI-debt fears rather than on any channel evidence: STM -27%, VICR -25%, NVTS -25%, IFX -20%, POWI -16%, ON -13%, TXN -11% over one month, with CNBC reporting chip stocks shedding over $1 trillion in the 7/29 rout. The last five sessions were a rebound (VICR +12%, NVTS +15%, AEIS +9%, IFX +9%), so this is a reset inside an uptrend, not a break — YTD is still +100% STM, +95% VICR, +76% LFUS/6723.T, +73% POWI. One thesis correction: last week's call that tightness was rotating to the 800VDC leg in 3Q26 is too early — Delta put 400V HVDC into mass production in Q3 2026 but 800V at only small volume in Q4 2026 with the real ramp in 2027, and Navitas said the same (hyperscaler 800V ramp 2027). The binding link this week is still the 12V/48V power stage and high-current PMIC, and inside that it has rotated from wafer to back-end: MPWR explicitly said its capacity problem is now module assembly and full-solution manufacturing, not silicon.

Company guided Q3 revenue to roughly +26% yoy on record bookings, and management said current data-center growth is tied to lower-voltage architectures with the shift to high-voltage architectures arriving in 2027 and beyond, where content per system is 2-4x higher. — Littelfuse Q2 2026 earnings release and call transcript (investor.littelfuse.com; Seeking Alpha transcript; Zacks) · 2026-07-29
  • Navitas Q2 2026 (reported 7/29): revenue $10.5M, +22% qoq, with high-power revenue up more than 50% yoy and non-GAAP gross margin 39.5%; company reported record book-to-bill and expanding backlog, guided Q3 to $13.5M +/- $0.5M (+28% qoq), and said 800V-architecture production samples are shipping with selected hyperscalers expected to ramp in 2027 — confirming the 800VDC volume step is a 2027, not 2026, event. — Navitas Semiconductor Q2 2026 8-K (SEC, tm2613492d1_ex99-1) and Semiconductor Today report · 2026-07-29

Rotation: The binding sub-link is still mature-node (8-inch BCD / power-discrete) capacity feeding 12V/48V-rail DrMOS power stages, high-current PMICs and MOSFETs — Delta, the largest merchant rack-PSU maker, named MOSFETs as fully constrained on 7/30, and Renesas confirmed the same tightness on 7/31 with sub-8-week channel inventory. It DID rotate this week, but inside the node rather than up the voltage stack: MPWR said on 7/30 that its capacity constraint is now complex module assembly and full-solution manufacturing rather than silicon wafers, so the marginal choke has migrated from front-end wafer to back-end module/assembly and test. Explicit correction to last week's read: the 800VDC leg is NOT yet binding. Delta put only 400V HVDC into mass production in Q3 2026, put 800V at small volume in Q4 2026 with the real ramp in 2027, and Navitas independently said hyperscaler 800V ramps in 2027 — so the 800V/SiC-GaN content story is a 2027 P&L event and today is a design-win and sampling story (MPWR only began sampling 800V AC-DC SiC parts this quarter). Structural caveat unchanged: low-end and automotive SiC remains in overcapacity while AI-grade high-current and high-voltage parts are short, so a headline 'SiC glut' is not evidence of easing here — onsemi notes 6-inch SiC substrate prices have bottomed and begun recovering. Mandate note: much of the marginal supply that sets lead times sits in Taiwan-listed hands (Delta 2308.TW, uPI Semi, Alpha & Omega, the passive vendors) — context only, so the exposure is routed to the EU/US/JP incumbents named below. First real physical relief still lands 2H27 (Vicor fab 2, AEIS Thailand from Q4 2026, Infineon Dresden module 4, Renesas' announced step-function supply increases).

Leading metric: Merchant-channel inventory in weeks plus book-to-bill at the power-stage vendors is now the cleanest forward gauge, because it moves before price and before lead time: Renesas is at under 8 weeks of sales-channel inventory (down from 12-14) and MPWR describes channel inventory as very low with book-to-bill well above 1.0x. The flip threshold: Renesas channel inventory rebuilding back above roughly 10-12 weeks, OR book-to-bill printing below 1.0 at two of MPWR / VICR / AEIS in the same quarter. Cross-check with quoted lead times — TrendForce has PMIC/VRM at 20-30 weeks with some PMICs stretched from 21-26 to 35-40 weeks and AI-server rush orders at 270 days versus a 180-day baseline; a sustained break back under ~180 days would confirm. Price-hike acceptance and the 10-25% July 1 increase round are now coincident, not leading.

I · Networking & OpticsPeak tightness
tightness 8/10priced 7/10neutralconf: medium

This was the week the substrate thesis stopped being an inference and became a cash-settled fact. On 7/29 Lumentum agreed to prepay AXT $87M in two $43.5M deposits to reserve indium-phosphide wafer capacity through 31 Dec 2031 — a five-year, cash-up-front lock on the exact sub-link the prior read named as binding. A day later AXT printed the corroborating economics: InP revenue a record $30.7M inside $47.6M total (+164% YoY), non-GAAP gross margin 45.0% versus 8.2% a year ago, backlog above $100M and management saying demand outpaces its ability to add capacity. A 37pp gross-margin swing is not a mix story, it is substrate pricing power. Simultaneously TrendForce's 7/27 release moved CPO from roadmap to production — NVIDIA's Spectrum-X CPO switch shipping to select partners, Broadcom's 51.2T Bailly in volume manufacture with Delta and Micas at Meta — and named a NEW parallel constraint: optical-engine yield, SiPh wafer fab and 2.5D/3D advanced packaging that now competes head-on with GPU/HBM for the same packaging queue. The offsetting change is on the priced axis, not the physical one: the whole node de-rated hard over three months (FN -37%, MRVL -43%, ALAB -38% from highs, LITE -20.6%) before bouncing 15-16% in the last five sessions on these datapoints. So tightness held while froth partially cleared — priced comes down a notch, but with LITE +105% and AXTI +316% YTD this remains a tight node that is mostly spent, with the residual asymmetry now sitting in the two or three names the drawdown left behind rather than in the node as a whole.

  • AXT entered a definitive agreement with Lumentum Operations LLC for supply and capacity reservation of indium-phosphide wafer substrates running through 31 December 2031; Lumentum pays an initial $43.5M deposit within 30 business days and a second $43.5M deposit with terms to be set during calendar 2028, both applied as shipment credits — an $87M cash prepayment to reserve substrate capacity five years forward. — AXT Inc Form 8-K / BusinessWire press release (businesswire.com/news/home/20260729446850) · 2026-07-29

  • TrendForce: NVIDIA has begun shipping its Spectrum-X CPO switch (co-developed with TSMC on COUPE packaging, up to 400 Tb/s) to select partners and Broadcom's 51.2T Bailly CPO switch has entered volume manufacturing with Delta Electronics and Micas Networks at Meta; TrendForce names optical-engine yield, silicon-photonics wafer fabrication and 2.5D/3D advanced packaging — which competes directly with AI chips and HPC processors for capacity — as the constraints setting the expansion pace, with the real volume ramp in 2027-2028. — TrendForce press release 20260727-13151 · 2026-07-27

Rotation: The binding sub-link did NOT rotate this week — it hardened in place at 6-inch InP substrate, and got the strongest possible confirmation: a customer paying $87M of cash five years ahead to reserve capacity, and the merchant supplier's gross margin going from 8.2% to 45.0% in four quarters with backlog above $100M. What did change is that a SECOND, parallel constraint opened one layer sideways. Per TrendForce on 7/27, CPO has entered production and its gating items are optical-engine yield, silicon-photonics wafer fab and 2.5D/3D advanced packaging — meaning the optics node now queues for the same advanced-packaging capacity as GPUs and HBM, linking NETOPT's constraint to the compute node's. A third, distinctly non-technical gate is now explicit: AXT's Q3 guide is scoped only to volumes "supported by existing export permits or non-restricted products," so Chinese export licensing on InP (controls imposed February 2025) is a live, quarter-by-quarter throttle on merchant substrate supply, and AXT's producing subsidiary Tongmei is Beijing-based. On the easing side the supply response is now enormous and dated: AXT doubling InP capacity in both 2026 and 2027 funded by a $632.5M raise, JX Advanced Metals spending up to JPY 120bn for 7-10x capacity, Lumentum EML units +50% by the December-2026 quarter, and Coherent's 6-inch InP conversion. The structural bear for the module/assembly layer is CPO displacing pluggables — but TrendForce dates real CPO volume at 2027-2028, so that is a 2027 problem, not a 2026 one. Taiwan-listed capacity adds (ELASER, LuxNet, Taiwan module assembly) and Chinese lines (Innolight 300308.SZ, Eoptolink, Yuanjie) remain material industry evidence but are context-only under the mandate and are never routed to a pick.

Leading metric: AXT's non-GAAP gross margin plus the count of customer capacity-reservation prepayments is now the cleanest forward gauge for the whole node, because it prices the binding sub-link directly rather than inferring it from module lead-times. Thresholds that flip the read: (a) BULLISH-CONFIRM if a second EML maker — Coherent, Broadcom or Sumitomo Electric — signs a prepaid InP substrate reservation before year-end, which would say the scramble is broad and not a single-customer idiosyncrasy; (b) PEAK-PASSED if AXT's non-GAAP gross margin stops climbing above the 45.0% just printed while its capacity doubles into 2027, because that is the signature of merchant substrate pricing power leaking. Cross-read against Lumentum FQ4-26 on 2026-08-11 (does the sold-out horizon still extend past CY2027, or merely hold?), Arista Q2 on 2026-08-04 (optics lead-times cited from the demand side), and Fabrinet FQ4 on 2026-08-17 (does back-end assembly bookings confirm or contradict the laser unlock?).

B · Servers / ODM / EMSConfirmed shortage
tightness 7/10priced 5/10bullconf: medium

This was the week the SERVER node's fundamentals and its share prices moved in opposite directions, which is the single most important thing to record. All three in-window prints raised: Celestica (Jul 27) took FY26 revenue to $20.5B from $19B and adj EPS to $11.30 from $10.15 on $4.70B/+62% revenue; Flex (Jul 29) raised FY27 to $33.7-35.2B with the Cloud & Power Infrastructure segment guided +65-75% and 90%+ of the next three quarters already booked; Sanmina (Jul 27) beat its own EPS guide by ~19% on $3.46B/+69.7% revenue. Meanwhile Meta lifted the floor of its 2026 capex range to $130-145B on Jul 29. Yet semis entered bear-market territory on Jul 28 (>20% off June highs) on circular-financing, CXMT and China-lithography fears, and this node's names now sit 27-52% below their 52-week highs — CLS $321 vs $474, JBL $314 vs $429, FLEX $114 vs $167, SANM $188 vs $289, SMCI $28.73 vs $59.40 at 8.5x forward. Tightness also got worse, not better: Celestica's CFO said plainly that "the constraint is really around material" rather than factory capacity, with lead times beyond 52 weeks on some parts versus the 40+ week marker the trade press carried three weeks ago, and customers now placing non-cancellable, non-refundable orders for long-lead silicon. So priced comes down hard (8 to 5) while tightness goes up (6 to 7) — the assembler layer is no longer a crowded re-rating trade, it is a de-rated one attached to raised numbers. The honest caveat is that this is a regime question above the node: if the "AI capex is peaking" narrative is right, raised FY26/FY27 guides are the last good quarters, and the second-order strain now visible in cash flow (Sanmina turned $3.46B of revenue into $24M of FCF) says the node is financing the shortage with its own balance sheet.

Rotation: The binding sub-link is unchanged in kind but materially worse in degree, and a genuinely new second link opened this week. Primary: component allocation UPSTREAM of the assembler — server DRAM/DDR5 RDIMM first, general-purpose CPU second — not assembly capacity. Celestica's CFO quantified it on 28 Jul as material rather than factory constraint, with 52+ week lead times on some parts, versus the publicly reported 40+ weeks on 16 Jul; suppliers have cut confirmed allocations roughly 30% and Tier-1 order books are filling at ~70%, with TrendForce looking for a further 13-18% q/q server-DRAM contract price rise in Q3 2026. Mizuho attributed Supermicro's FQ4 revenue landing at the LOW end of its $11.0-12.5B range to component/memory shortage and shell-construction delays even as orders exceeded $60B — that is the shortage converting demand into unshipped backlog, which is the cleanest proof the constraint binds. NEW this week and now arguably the node's own binding link: working capital and balance-sheet capacity to carry allocated inventory. Sanmina converted $3.46B of revenue into $24M of free cash flow and explicitly warned working capital rises with AI ramps; Celestica's inventory is $3.4B, up $1.5B y/y; analysts flagged that Supermicro's $60B backlog may require fresh debt or equity. The rotation is therefore component allocation PLUS financing capacity — cash to pre-buy memory at spot-linked prices — and it separates pass-through/consignment contract structures (Jabil, Flex CPI) from turnkey structures (Supermicro, and Dell whose AI server gross margin compressed to ~18% from ~21%) far more than it separates factories. Structural overhang to keep flagging: Nvidia supplying fully assembled L10 compute trays (~90% of server cost) shrinks the ODM to rack integration and power, capping value capture. The true rack-assembly constraint owners remain Taiwan-listed (Quanta 2382.TW ~25-30% share, Hon Hai 2317.TW ~40%, Wistron 3231.TW, Wiwynn 6669.TW) and are CONTEXT ONLY under mandate, so the tradable expression continues to route to the US-listed EMS layer — and specifically to the power/cooling content that monetizes the NEXT rotation of the bottleneck rather than the memory one.

Leading metric: Allocated server-DRAM (DDR5 RDIMM) contract lead time in weeks, now 52+ on some parts per Celestica's CFO (up from the 40+ week figure carried in mid-July trade press), read together with supplier fill rates (~70% of Tier-1 order books being filled after suppliers cut confirmed allocations ~30%). Flip thresholds: (a) BEAR-flip if lead times extend past ~60 weeks AND any Tier-1 ODM or OEM formally cuts a shipment or revenue guide because of it — at that point scarcity stops being accretive pass-through and starts destroying the node's revenue; (b) BULL-exhaustion flip if lead times fall back under ~30 weeks with fill rates above 90%, which removes the node's pricing power and pass-through economics. Secondary gauge specific to this node: free cash flow conversion — Sanmina's $24M FCF on $3.46B of revenue is the number to watch; if CLS, SANM or SMCI post another quarter of sub-2% FCF/revenue while inventory keeps building, the node is funding the shortage rather than profiting from it.

DEPLOYMENT — Can the compute be physically built, powered & cooled? (cooling, power equipment, grid, permits)

J · Liquid Cooling / ThermalPeak tightness
tightness 8/10priced 5/10bullconf: medium

Three of the node's five leaves reported in five days and all three said the same thing: they cannot physically ship what they have already sold. Vertiv (7/29) MISSED revenue at $3.274B vs $3.39B consensus on "temporary supply chain congestion" yet raised FY26 guidance across every metric (adj EPS to $6.65-6.75, +60%); Modine (7/29) grew Data Centers +90% to $348.6M but took a 960bp DC gross-margin hit to 20.2% because supply constraints "limited production"; Munters had already flagged the same ramp/supply margin drag on 7/17. Revenue misses and margin compression at the OEM level while orders and guidance rise is the classic PEAK-TIGHTNESS signature, not a demand problem — so I am moving the stage from confirmed_shortage to peak_tightness, with the constraint now sitting one tier UPSTREAM of the CDU/chiller makers (components, plant ramp, overhead absorption) rather than in their own order books. The first genuine roll-signal also appeared: nVent's backlog slipped to $2.5B from $2.6B and organic order growth decelerated from ~40% in Q1 to low-double-digit in Q2, the cohort's first sequential backlog decline this cycle — though nVent simultaneously raised FY26 adj EPS to $5.00-5.10, guided data-center sales above $2B (2x last year), and announced its third liquid-cooling plant in three years. Meanwhile the equities de-rated FURTHER into 7/31 against rising numbers: VRT closed 7/31 at $241.57, -36% from its high and roughly 36x a just-raised FY26 EPS versus ~50x five weeks ago, and MOD broke below its 200-day for the first time this cycle at $201.06. That multiple compression against upgraded guidance is why priced comes down 6 to 5 even as tightness holds at 8. The sharpest contrast is geographic: the Taiwan cold-plate volume owners are NOT de-rated at all — AVC (3017.TW, context-only) closed +9.9% today at NT$2,550, just 15% off its high — so the US/EU CDU and chiller names are now the cheaper way to own the identical physical shortage.

Rotation: The binding sub-link ROTATED THIS WEEK, one tier upstream and inside the node. It is no longer the CDU/chiller OEMs' own order books or fabrication footprint — it is the component and ramp tier feeding them: pumps, compressors, heat exchangers, coils, controls/electronics, plus skilled plant labor and overhead absorption on brand-new lines. The proof is that three independent issuers hit the same wall in the same week without coordinating: Vertiv missed revenue on "supply chain congestion" while raising margin guidance, Modine's Data Centers gross margin fell 960bp to 20.2% because constraints "limited production," and Munters' DCT margin came in at 11.3% vs 12.8% expected on supply-chain and ramp inefficiency. When suppliers are losing margin to a shortage rather than harvesting it, tightness is at or very near its maximum. Three secondary observations. (a) The 2025 pinch — cold plates, manifolds and quick-disconnects — has clearly loosened; the volume owners are Taiwan-listed (AVC 3017.TW; Auras, correctly 3324.TWO on the Taipei Exchange, not 3324.TW) and are CONTEXT-ONLY under this mandate, and their share prices sit near highs rather than reflecting scarcity rents. The listed Western proxies for the QD tier are Parker-Hannifin (PH, NYSE, Stäubli/Parker connector overlap) and Dover (DOV, NYSE, owner of CPC), both verified live but far too diversified to be pure-play alpha, so neither is named. (b) The pacing item for actual megawatt commissioning has moved further downstream into mechanical install labor and modular prefab floor space — Comfort Systems' record $14.06B backlog (7/23) and 4M-to-5M sq ft modular expansion is that link, and it is fully allocated to named customers. (c) The dashboard-level binding constraint remains POWER/GRID, not COOL: capacity being poured into chiller lines and liquid-cooling plants through 2027 (nVent's third plant in three years, Modine's Grenada chiller lines, Vertiv capex at 4% of sales) will increasingly meet an interconnection wall rather than a fabrication wall. Net: COOL is at maximum tightness right now but its SHARE of the marginal constraint has begun to decline, and nVent's backlog roll is the first hard evidence of that.

Leading metric: Two-part gauge, and the second half is NEW this week. (1) ORDER SIDE: cohort organic order growth / book-to-bill. nVent has already tripped my prior threshold — organic orders decelerated from ~+40% (Q1) to low-double-digit (Q2) and backlog fell $2.6B to $2.5B, the first sequential decline in the cohort. One name is not a trend; the flip is CONFIRMED if a SECOND leaf posts a sequential backlog decline or sub-1.0x book-to-bill (next reads: Munters Q3 late October, Vertiv Q3 late October, Comfort Systems Q3 late October). (2) MARGIN SIDE, the cleaner tightness read now: data-center segment margin at the OEMs, because shortage currently shows up as unshippable revenue and overhead under-absorption rather than as order weakness. Modine's DC adjusted EBITDA margin is the single cleanest number — 14.8% this quarter vs 22.1% a year ago, with management guiding 19-20% for fiscal Q2 (reports late Oct/early Nov). If it prints back at or above 19-20%, the component constraint has cleared and shortage-driven pricing power starts fading (bullish for EPS, bearish for the shortage thesis); if it prints below ~16% again, the shortage is deeper and longer than the OEMs think and the peak-tightness call extends into 2027.

Distributor lead-time (live, computed): COOL-FAN 37wk · 2/2 parts tight (DigiKey+Mouser) — the hard 2nd source: a real shortage shows up here before it prints in revenue.

K · Power Equipment / Grid / DC DeploymentConfirmed shortage
tightness 9/10priced 7/10bullconf: medium

This was the node's densest earnings week of the year, and for the first time in months the physical read got tighter while the equity read got cheaper. Eaton (07-31) printed Electrical Americas orders +41% organic on a rolling-12 basis, Electrical Americas backlog of $15.2B (+33% y/y, +$3.8B) at a 1.3 book-to-bill, and an Electrical Global backlog that MORE than doubled (+103% y/y) at 1.2 book-to-bill, then raised FY organic growth 200bps to 11-13%. Quanta (07-30) took total backlog to a record $53.4B from $43.98B at year-end 2025 on $9.56B of quarterly revenue versus $6.77B a year ago. The cleanest tightness tell was Vertiv (07-29): it beat on margin (22.6% adjusted, +410bps) and raised full-year sales guidance by $250M, yet landed ~$76M BELOW its own revenue guidance midpoint, explicitly on "temporary supply chain congestion and multi-phased project execution" — the constraint is now eating revenue recognition inside the OEMs, not merely stretching quoted lead times. Against that, the node de-rated hard through July (VRT -19.3% 1M, MYRG -23.4%, POWL -14.0%, GEV -12.9%) before bouncing into these prints, so priced comes down from 9 to 7: still not cheap (ETN ~32x, VRT ~38x, PWR ~40x forward on raised guidance) but no longer pinned at highs. The one genuinely new bear input is demand-side, not supply-side: Exelon (07-30) cut its total data-center pipeline ~16% to ~36 GW and its "high-probability" pipeline ~40% to 11 GW after screening speculative requests through transmission service agreements, while still reaffirming its $41B five-year capital plan — phantom-load pruning, not a capex cut. PJM's board separately proposed a backstop capacity auction plus explicit data-center curtailment mechanisms (07-28), institutionalizing rationing as the normal way the grid clears rather than an emergency measure. Net: tightness holds at 9, priced falls to 7, and the asymmetry that was spent last week has partially re-opened — but via multiple compression, not via any new deterioration in supply.
