Rotation: The binding sub-link rotated AGAIN this week, and in an unusual direction. Last week it had moved from the iron (LPT, MV switchgear, GOES, bushings, HV breakers) downstream to ENERGIZATION — interconnection queues, PJM capacity adequacy, DOE curtailment authority. That energization link hardened further: PJM's board proposed a backstop capacity auction plus explicit data-center curtailment plans on 07-28, making rationing a standing market design feature rather than a heat-wave emergency, and ClearView flagged that FERC's response will drive utility capital investment and data-center development timelines. But a NEW binding sub-link appeared inside the OEMs themselves: Vertiv missed its own revenue guide by ~$76M on 'temporary supply chain congestion,' meaning the shortage has now crossed from order books into revenue recognition — the equipment makers cannot convert their own backlog on schedule. The labor leg simultaneously confirmed pricing power: MYR Group's Q2 (07-29) put backlog at a record $3.16B (+19.6% y/y, T&D $1.27B / C&I $1.89B) with C&I revenue a record $557.7M (+$163.6M y/y) and gross margin 13.2% versus 11.5% a year ago — scarce electrical field crews are repricing. The counterweight is that the demand leg is being pruned for the first time: Exelon cut high-probability data-center load ~40% to 11 GW while keeping its $41B capex plan, and PG&E is vetting a 12.7 GW pipeline more strictly, even as FirstEnergy went the other way (data-center contracted + pipeline demand +30% q/q, contracted 6.4 GW, West Virginia +137% to 4.3 GW) and CenterPoint sees ~14 GW eligible for Texas 'Batch Zero' with ~50% load growth by end-2029. No Taiwan-listed name owns this node's binding link. Taiwan exposure here is downstream and CONTEXT ONLY — Delta Electronics (2308.TW) in power conversion/busway, and Vertiv's own Q2 deck flagged Taiwan as a demand site (the VisionBay.AI K1 800VDC project in Kaohsiung and Taiwan's first NVIDIA GB300 AI data center), i.e. Taiwan is a customer of this constraint, not the owner of it. The real owners remain US/EU/JP/KR: GEV, Eaton, ABB, Siemens Energy, Hitachi Energy (via 6501.T), Fuji Electric, HD Hyundai Electric.

Leading metric: Eaton's Electrical Americas rolling-12 book-to-bill together with the absolute Electrical Americas backlog dollar level, now 1.3x and $15.2B. Flip threshold: the read turns from bull to neutral the first quarter EA book-to-bill prints below 1.1 while backlog stops growing sequentially, and turns bear below 1.0 with a sequential backlog decline — that is the mechanical point at which quoted delivery slots stop sliding right. Secondary demand-side confirm, which is where the crack would show up first this cycle: the aggregate 'high-probability / contracted' data-center GW disclosed by PJM- and ERCOT-zone utilities (Exelon now 11 GW after a ~40% cut, FirstEnergy 6.4 GW contracted, CenterPoint ~14 GW Batch-Zero-eligible, PG&E 12.7 GW). If two more large utilities cut high-probability pipelines by more than 20% next quarter while equipment book-to-bill is still above 1, the shortage is demand-deflating rather than supply-resolving, and the OEM backlog quality — not backlog size — becomes the thing to underwrite.

MONETIZATION / MARGIN — Does the build convert to profit, or is there overbuild / margin risk? (capex-conversion, GPU cloud pricing, supplier GM, inventory/backlog quality)

Margin · Capex-Conversion & Margin Risk (cross-cut)Peak tightness
tightness 8/10priced 7/10neutralconf: medium

The node's two halves moved in opposite directions this week, and that split is the whole story. Supplier margin risk is now decisively disproved: Vertiv's 2026-07-29 Q2 printed a 22.6% adjusted operating margin (+410bp y/y) and raised every FY26 line — operating margin to 23.3-24.3% from 22.5%, adjusted EPS to $6.65-6.75 from $6.30-6.40 — layered on top of SMCI's earlier gross-margin revision to 15-17%. Buyer financial capacity deteriorated on exactly the schedule Epoch AI modelled: Amazon's 2026-07-30 Q2 converted the capex-over-operating-cash-flow crossover from forecast into reported fact, with TTM operating cash flow of $161.4B against ~$169B of TTM capex and free cash flow swinging from +$18.2B to -$7.6B. Microsoft's answer was not to spend less but to depreciate slower — extending data-center and office useful life from 15 to 25 years effective FY27, which also reclassifies finance leases into operating leases and cuts headline CY2026 capex from ~$190B to ~$175B with no change to real outlay — and the market paid it roughly $260B of market cap for the quarter. Credit kept repricing the risk the equity ignored: Oracle's 5-year CDS traded near 200bp on 2026-07-29 versus a 53bp investment-grade index, with the rest of the hyperscaler complex near 49bp, its highest since 2018. The honest early-vs-priced read is that the margin-compression bear case is now fully priced OUT of the OEMs — DELL is +234% YTD at $417.33 and HPE +105% at $49.01 — while the financing bear case is priced IN at Oracle, down 23% YTD and sitting near its 52-week low. What is not priced is earnings quality: a depreciation-life extension was rewarded rather than discounted. The node is genuinely two-sided — demand conversion improved (AWS +37%, fastest in 18 quarters; Azure through $100B with FY27 guided ~45% cc) even as the balance sheets funding it visibly weakened.

  • Amazon Q2 2026 confirmed the capex-over-cash-flow crossover: trailing-twelve-month operating cash flow $161.4B (+33% y/y) against ~$169B of TTM capex (+64% y/y), with TTM free cash flow swinging from +$18.2B a year earlier to an outflow of $7.6B on a $66.1B y/y increase in purchases of property and equipment. The change also shifts more future data-center leases from finance leases (which count in reported capex) to operating leases (which do not), cutting headline CY2026 capex guidance from roughly $190B to about $175B with no change to underlying spending plans. Rotation: The binding sub-link did not rotate this week; it deepened and grew a second head. It remains the buyer's balance sheet rather than supplier gross margin — that rotation happened last week, and Vertiv's 22.6% margin with a raised guide confirms the supplier link is loose, not binding. What is new is that the constraint now expresses itself through accounting as well as credit: Microsoft chose to extend data-center useful life from 15 to 25 years rather than trim spend, which lowers future depreciation and moves finance leases (inside reported capex) into operating leases (outside it), cutting headline CY2026 capex ~$190B to ~$175B without cutting a dollar of real outlay. The binding question has therefore moved from "can suppliers earn a margin" (answered: yes, decisively) to "are hyperscaler reported operating margins real, and can the buyers fund the next leg without impairing credit." Oracle is the stress proxy — BBB- since 2026-07-09, FY27 capex forecast $90-95B, projected free operating cash flow -$42B, CDS ~200bp, equity -23% YTD near its 52-week low of $199.19 — and Amazon is the newly-crossed case at -$7.6B TTM free cash flow, though its equity is making new 52-week highs on accelerating AWS. Profit-pool migration is unchanged in direction: away from compute-rental resellers, toward memory/component suppliers (Microsoft's own ~$25B capex attribution to component inflation) and physical-infrastructure oligopolies. Honesty flag, repeated from last week: the largest single owner of AI-server assembly margin is the Taiwan ODM complex — Quanta 2382.TW, Wistron 3231.TW, Hon Hai 2317.TW — which is context-only under the no-Taiwan rule, so exposure is routed to US-listed lines sharing the same mix inflection (VRT, SMCI, and the now-priced DELL/HPE). Next catalyst inside the node: SMCI's full FY26 Q4 print on 2026-08-11.

Leading metric: Oracle 5-year CDS in basis points, read alongside the ex-Oracle hyperscaler CDS composite. Oracle went from ~125bp at the prior read to ~200bp on 2026-07-29 against a 53bp IG index. Thresholds that flip the read: a sustained break above ~250bp, or — the more important tell — the ex-Oracle hyperscaler composite breaking above ~75bp from ~49bp today, would mean the stress has generalized from an Oracle/OpenAI-concentration story into a sector-wide funding constraint, which is the mechanism that actually forces capex-guidance cuts. Conversely Oracle back below ~150bp alongside a well-covered new issue flips it bullish. Secondary gauge with a hard trigger: a SECOND hyperscaler following Microsoft's 15-to-25-year useful-life extension would confirm that reported operating margins cannot absorb true asset lives — treat that as bearish regardless of what credit is doing. Both credit and depreciation policy lead reported margins by roughly 2-3 quarters; gross-margin prints are coincident-to-lagging.

Best IBKR alpha: VRT NYSE (Class A) — the one name in the node where earnings went UP and price went · SMCI Nasdaq — the widest gap in the node between disclosed news and price. Record $60

Analytical lens computed from the other nodes — flags overbuild/margin deterioration, the bearish counter-signal to raw tightness.

L3 chokepoint ranking: subsegments ranked by computed net pressure = L3 tightness − 0.6 × priced. Hard distributor feeds override the computed score where available.

Most important watch rule (framework): the best leading indicator is usually not the obvious AI component. After GPU/HBM/CoWoS, the bottleneck rotates to second- and third-order parts — CPUs, MLCCs, inductors, VRMs/power-stages, retimers, optical modules, cold-plates, CDUs, transformers, substations, grid interconnection. Keep asking, of each: what's growing faster than qualified supply, and the market hasn't priced it yet? — right now that answer is Power Equipment / Grid / DC Deployment + Power Silicon.

How this dashboard reviews & evolves itself

Each weekly run, an independent Codex agent (gpt-5.5, highest effort) role-plays a skeptical IBKR-only retail investor and scores this dashboard on six dimensions, logging the verdict so the tool tracks — and works down — its own weaknesses. The research + data layers refresh automatically; flagged structural changes enter the queue below.

Method & honest caveats

Two data layers. (1) Computed (free, deterministic, reruns weekly): SEC EDGAR XBRL revenue-acceleration + gross-margin trend + inventory/RPO (point-in-time via filing date), Yahoo 52-week position + relative-strength vs ^SOX, live DigiKey + Mouser part-number lead-times for the catalog-distributable nodes (power silicon now up to 61.5wk, MLCC/passives ~24wk, retimers ~26wk, memory modules ~30wk), public cloud GPU rental pricing from Lambda/RunPod/Azure, LBNL interconnection queue requested/IA-executed MW for system-level GRID stress, Texas TDLR/TABS data-center project registrations plus a derived utility follow-up watchlist for site-level construction evidence and next MW/service-date proof targets, a source-linked transformer supply rowset, and a source-linked ISO/utility large-load rowset for direct data-center/load evidence. The estimates scaffold validates consensus_estimates.csv, FMP prior earnings events, FMP earnings-calendar timing rows, and FMP analyst-awareness rows, but current-snapshot / timing / recognition-stage rows do not unlock the PIT action gate. Nodes with no free exact feed yet (HBM contract price, CoWoS/ABF capacity, CPU attach/availability) carry research-sourced leading metrics instead, flagged as such. (2) Research (web, sourced, per subsector + SemiAnalysis article/ChipBook-derived metadata): an independent agent verifies tickers, assigns the shortage stage, and pulls recent sourced evidence. SemiAnalysis rows use article titles/URLs and derived classifications; if subscription body text is used, it is transient and not saved. This layer is refreshed weekly and adversarially self-reviewed by Codex (gpt-5.5, highest effort) role-playing a retail investor.

Caveats — do not over-trust. (1) The "priced-in" axis is a price-position proxy, not a valuation model; with ^SOX +101%/6mo the whole sector is extended, so COMPUTED-INFLECTION names are relatively un-run, not cheap. (2) US quarterly fundamentals lag a monthly channel-check; the live edge is the distributor lead-time + the research layer, not the 10-Q. (3) Curated stages carry a confidence flag — low-confidence nodes are hypotheses. (4) Taiwan-listed names (Tai-Tech, GUC, Alchip, Quanta, Yageo, Unimicron) are the true pure-plays in several nodes but are not in your IBKR access — they are shown for completeness and routed to a tradable proxy; the proxy is an imperfect substitute. (5) This is a discovery/triage map, NOT a buy list — every candidate needs an identity + AI-revenue-purity + valuation check before sizing. (6) IBKR is READ-ONLY; nothing here is investment advice.

Coverage now: 109 leaf-positions across 12 subsectors · 101 IBKR-tradable · 24 tradable COMPUTED-INFLECTION candidates · 0 action-gated EARLY-ENTRY · research layer LIVE (12/12).
